| conj:level-one-fresh-block-conservativity | Over F2, a fresh accuracy-one block with affine inputs adds no degree-D consequence in the existing variables when the base is Boolean equations plus complete accuracy-one affine blocks, at any rank | conjecture | [Checks and statement](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#level-one-conservativity-conjecture), [dual raw-coefficient-moment form](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#raw-coefficient-moment-dual) |
| audit:fresh-block-losses-placement | The one-level theorem dualizes to a weighted-substitution design; the fresh-block losses are finite instances of second-level inputs that are sums of low flat indicators, the recorded composition frontier | context | [Review](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#review-weighted-substitution-as-design), [placement](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#review-fresh-block-placement), [next step](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#review-selected-next-step) |
| obs:third-level-rank-two-map-obstruction | The combined rank-two old-only map does not extend to a general third level: a third-level input reading a low-pair parent with branch rank below v ln(8M)/k is a perturbed-hyperplane indicator that the kernel count cannot absorb, and bottom sums are outside the quotient lemma | context | [Inputs at the weight](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#third-level-map-at-the-weight), [obstruction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#third-level-obstruction), [restricted ledger](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#third-level-restricted-ledger) |
| obs:middle-rank-frontier | Matching restriction cannot make dense low-rank forms constant on any polynomial residual, and (claim corrected in `cor:polylog-high-threshold-cube-volume`) enlarging the weight degree was said not to lower the high threshold; the open zone is a level-two block of small affine rank reading many distinct low blocks, or a middle-rank block reading any low block | context; corrects by cor:polylog-high-threshold-cube-volume (reviewed) | [Restriction obstruction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#low-block-restriction-obstruction), [rebalancing failure](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#weight-degree-rebalancing-fails), [frontier](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#middle-rank-frontier) |
| lem:flat-restriction-dimension | The restriction of multilinear polynomials of degree <= k to a flat of codimension r has dimension <= sum_{j<=k} C(v-r,j) <= 2 e^{-rk/v} C(v,k) for k <= (v-r)/3 | working_proof | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#flat-restriction-dimension) |
| cor:polylog-high-threshold-cube-volume | In the cube-volume framework with k = floor((n/4-D)/(2D+1)) and D >= max(13, ell), affine tuples of rank >= v ln(16M)/k = O(D ell log M) are clamped by the weight and all others are packed by one-bin telescoping; the survivors on the weight's support are narrow terms | conditional; corrects by audit:residual-window-degree-ledger (reviewed) | [Corollary and checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#polylog-high-threshold), [frontier](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#narrow-term-frontier) |
| imp:beame-q-matching-switching-lemma | Over qn+1 points, an r-disjunction restricted by a random partial q-matching leaving q ell+1 points has canonical matching decision tree of height >= s with probability < (4 e^q r^{1/q} p^q n^{q-1/q})^s, for 6 <= ell = pn <= (n/r)^{1/q^2}/e | established | [Statement and locator](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#switching-lemma-imported-statement) |
| lem:switched-reader-old-image | A matching decision tree of height s that represents and refines a disjunction of terms of size <= r yields a complete old-only image of the ENS block reading those terms: product and coefficient images of degree <= s, companions through r+s, coefficient identity through s, Booleanity through 2s, over point axioms, exclusions, and Boolean equations | conditional; corrects by obs:two-sided-switching-not-functional-image (reviewed) | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#switched-parent-old-image) |
| cor:literal-term-readers-polylog-images | With ell ~ n^{1/4} r^{-1/4} and s = ceil(log2(2M)), a common restriction gives all M readers of width-r literal terms images with certificates through r + 2 log2(2M), on a residual of Theta(n^{1/4} r^{-1/4}) points | working_proof | [Parameters and scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#switched-reader-parameters) |
| lem:bipartite-matching-switching | On the (n+1) x n board, for an r-disjunction and 1 <= s <= ell <= n-1, the fraction of size-(n-ell) partial matchings whose canonical matching decision tree has height >= s is at most (ell+1)^s sum_{j>=s/2} A^j <= 2((ell+1) sqrt(A))^s, A = 4 r ell (ell+1)/((n-ell+1) ln 2) <= 1/2 | working_proof; corrects by obs:two-sided-switching-not-functional-image (reviewed) | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-switching-setting), [lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-switching-lemma), [parameters and evidence](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-switching-parameters) |
| lem:bit-term-decoder-image | A conjunction of r bit literals on rho rows decodes, modulo I_r(F^fun), to the sum of the at most n^rho pairwise incompatible unary terms assigning each row a consistent hole | working_proof | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bit-term-decoder-image) |
| lem:sum-input-switched-image | With inputs that are sums of pairwise incompatible unary terms and a representing, refining tree of height s, full leaf terms as coefficient images give companions and the coefficient identity through r+s and Booleanity through 2s | working_proof | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#sum-input-switched-image) |
| obs:restriction-family-obstruction | The switching lemma's extension count needs uniform partial matchings, while the weight step needs the compact subcube family with a fixed residual subcube; in that family extensions give ratio C(N,j)(N+1)_j >= 1, and after a uniform restriction the residual is not affine; unary weight fails by ambient dimension mn | context | [Obstruction and openings](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#restriction-family-obstruction) |
| lem:subcube-bit-switching | In the slack subcube family Phi_e (N+1+e residual pigeons, e free outside labels, fixed residual subcube of size N), a reader with <= r rows per term and <= w literals per row, w <= l-l_2-1, e >= 2^{w+2} s, has canonical-tree height >= s with probability <= A^s + M r exp(-e 2^{-w-4}), A = 2r(N+e)e(N+e+1)/(n-N-e+1); the tree transfers to the compact family Phi_0; with e=N, N^3 <= n/(48r), w <= log2 N - log2 s - 6, s = ceil(log2(8M)) one restriction serves all readers | working_proof | [Lemma, proof, parameters](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#subcube-switching-lemma) |
| ex:fixed-subcube-pinned-rows-refuted | For the fixed subcube Q, the reader OR_i [row i pinned to q_{i mod N}] (n+1 one-row terms of l literals, rank l <= r_high) has, for all but an N^{-1/10} fraction of Phi_0, no matching decision tree of height below N/(2 log2 N + 35) whose leaves falsify every term or contain a satisfied term | refutation | [Claim and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#subcube-switching-refutation-fixed) |
| obs:subcube-code-obstruction | An extension encoding in any family whose residual holes form a flat gains only from the residual pigeons and can record a residual row only by matching it to a consistent free label outside the flat, so it covers exactly rows with at most about log2(e/s) literals; a uniformly random flat defeats the pinned-row reader and keeps the lemma, leaving row widths between (1/3) log2 n and (c-1/3) log2 n open for M = n^c | context | [Sections 4 and 5](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#subcube-switching-code-obstruction) |
| lem:random-flat-restriction-preservation | Restricting to a uniformly random affine flat Q of dimension l_2 (live rows uniform, others matched bijectively outside Q) maps degree-d old-bit polynomials to degree <= d in the residual bits, maps Q_n into the ideal of Q_N with certificates through l, and keeps the affine decoder consistent modulo the residual row axiom; the multiplier space, flat count, and cube detector apply verbatim on Q | working_proof | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#random-flat-preservation) |
| thm:random-flat-literal-switching | In the random-flat family, a reader whose light part (rows below w_0 literals) satisfies the subcube switching hypotheses has, with probability >= 1 - A^s - M r exp(-e 2^{-w-4}) - M_h r (N+1+e)(N+2r) 2^{-w_0}/n, a tree of height < s whose 1-leaves contain a satisfied term and whose 0-leaves falsify every light term while heavy terms are falsified or vanish modulo residual Booleanity; hence complete switching for sources of size S <= n/(768 s r (N+2r)); refined on 2026-09-18 (`entry-2026-09-18-literal-window`) to (N+1) in place of (N+1+e), giving completeness up to n^{4/3}/polylog and a window from (1/3) log2 n to (c-1) log2 n for S = n^c, c > 4/3 | working_proof | [Theorem, proof, parameters](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#random-flat-heavy-dichotomy) |
| ex:q-skip-tree-encoding-refuted | Making the canonical tree skip terms with a row that has no consistent unused hole of Q breaks the extension encoding: a moved row's consistent outside code label can revive a skipped term for the decoder | refutation | [Section 5](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#q-skip-refuted) |
| lem:hole-query-node-functional | A hole-query node with branches X_{jq} over uncovered pigeons and the empty branch u_q = 1 - sum_j X_{jq} has branch factors summing to one identically, pairwise products zero modulo Boolean and exclusion axioms at degree two, and idempotent u_q; trees with such nodes keep leaf degree <= height, the leaf partition identity, and pairwise incompatibility, so the switched-reader image lemmas extend verbatim without any onto axiom | working_proof | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-query-nodes) |
| obs:literal-window-structure | The light range (1/3) log2 n is intrinsic to extension encodings in flat families (the free-set entropy is the gain; a fixed free pool only raises the family ratio); a window term is alive-unsatisfied with a Q-consistent heavy row with probability <= r 4^r (N+1)N/n times the product of its pattern densities, against >= 2^{-2r-1} times the product for satisfaction, so the window matters only for readers with correlated satisfaction events; the sufficient obligation is a bound on >= k_0 alive window terms with distinct heavy rows and no satisfied term (corrected on 2026-09-18 by `ex:random-flat-pigeon-only-refuted`: the holes must also be distinct, and the tree must use hole queries) | context; corrects by ex:random-flat-pigeon-only-refuted (reviewed) | [Section 3](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#window-structure) |
| ex:random-flat-pigeon-only-refuted | In the random-flat family the single-target reader F_{x*} = OR_i [row i pinned to x*] is, with probability N/n (the event x* in Q), unsatisfied and without any pigeon-only deciding tree of height < N-1, while one hole query at x* decides it; the n readers F_{x*} (size n^2) contain a bad one for every restriction | refutation | [Claim and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pigeon-only-random-flat-refutation) |
| obs:alive-pair-matching-obligation | For unary heavy rows the alive pinned pairs (residual row, label in Q) form a bipartite graph B; a tree decides the pinned part in height equal to the vertex cover number = matching number nu(B) (König), and no tree does better when no term is satisfied; the readers OR_x [i_0 -> x] and F_{x*} show that distinct holes alone or distinct rows alone do not suffice; dense pinned classes (>= n ln(8SK) pairs) are satisfied by matched edges when their tail is (negative association of a uniform bijection, imported from Joag-Dev and Proschan 1983), sparse classes have few alive pairs in expectation; the missing step is an exponential tail for nu(B) over the random flat | context | [Sections 1 and 2](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#alive-pair-graph) |
| lem:dense-labels-satisfied | Given (Q, R'), if at least Delta_0 = N + 2(n-N) ln(8S)/D* labels have degree >= D* >= 2(N+1) in a unary reader, some term is satisfied by the uniform bijection mu' with probability >= 1 - 1/(8S) | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by lem:dense-labels-satisfied-with-slack (reviewed) | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#dense-labels-satisfied) |
| lem:sparse-matching-tail | Alive pinned pairs with labels of degree < D* = k_1(n-k_1)/(2e(N+1)N) have matching number >= k_1 with probability <= 2^{-k_1}, by the symmetric sum of degrees over the flat's N points and the uniform residual set | working_proof | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#sparse-matching-tail) |
| lem:dense-labels-in-flat | A set D of labels meets a uniformly random flat of size N in >= k_2 points with probability <= \|D\|^{k_2} (N/n)^{ceil(log2 k_2)+1} | working_proof | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#dense-labels-in-flat) |
| thm:unary-reader-hole-query-switching | In the random-flat family a unary reader has, with probability >= 1 - 1/(8S) - Delta_0^{k_2}(N/n)^{ceil(log2 k_2)+1} - 2^{-k_1}, a pigeon-and-hole-query tree of height <= k_1 + k_2 whose 1-leaves contain a satisfied term and whose 0-leaves falsify or annihilate every term; for S = n^c readers, k_1 = ceil(log2(16S)), k_2 = 2^{c+2}, one restriction serves all unary readers with height O(log S + 2^c); readers with separate light and unary terms are covered with the light encoding | conditional; corrects by thm:unary-reader-switching-with-slack (reviewed) | [Theorem and corollaries](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#unary-switching-theorem) |
| lem:adaptive-heavy-query-rule | In the canonical tree for mixed readers, querying the pigeon when its row has at least two unkilled pairs of B_0 (residual row, label in Q, tails ignored) and otherwise the hole makes every path's heavy queries pairwise distinct in rows and holes, hence at most nu(B_t) with B_t the pairs whose tail is not falsified; the decoder recomputes the rule from the code, so the light encoding applies with heavy queries recorded unmoved | refutation; corrects by ex:adaptive-rule-pigeon-counterexample (reviewed); corrects by lem:both-endpoints-rule (reviewed); corrects by lem:hole-only-rule (reviewed) | [Lemma and decomposition](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#adaptive-heavy-query-rule) |
| lem:matched-first-preference | Preferring, among non-falsified terms, the earliest whose pinned pair is matched makes every heavy query come from a class with no matched pinned pair, so the heavy queries of a path number at most nu(B_um), the matching number of alive pairs of unmatched classes with non-falsified tails; decoder-computable | working_proof | [Lemma and analysis](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#matched-first-preference) |
| obs:window-height-budget | The residual weight step needs image degrees 2s+r <= N/4, so tree height N/8-r suffices for the window and N is a free polylogarithmic parameter; heavy queries number at most 2N+1 on any path, so every literal reader switches at every polynomial size with height 6N+3c, and the open mixed-term obligation is a constant-factor bound (at most N/24 heavy queries on paths with few light answers) | conditional; corrects by audit:residual-window-degree-ledger (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Budget, trivial count, sharpened obligation](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#window-height-budget) |
| lem:alive-unmatched-comparison | For any pair set P in the random-flat family, Pr[some pair alive and none matched] <= 45(N+1)^2 ln(n)/n * Pr[some pair matched]; no structure of P or of the flat is used | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed) | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#alive-unmatched-comparison) |
| lem:tail-comparison | A light tail on at most r-1 rows is alive but not satisfied by the restriction with probability at most 2(r-1)2^w(N+1)/n times the probability that it is satisfied, also conditionally on the other rows and the flat | working_proof | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#tail-comparison) |
| cor:eligibility-expectation | For any mixed reader, the expected number of classes eligible to trigger a heavy query is at most theta'(1+eps_w) times the expected number of classes with a satisfied term, theta' = 45(N+1)^2 ln(n)/n (1+o(1)) | working_proof | [Corollary and obstacle](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#eligibility-corollary) |
| thm:single-row-full-class-switching | Mixed readers whose classes are full (all rows pinned to one label) with single-row light tails on pairwise distinct rows have, at every polynomial source size n^c, at most 2^(2c+2) heavy queries on every path except with probability n^(-c-1)+n^(-2c-2): many labels force a satisfied term by negative association over distinct rows, few labels bound the trigger holes by the affine rank of the label set in the flat | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#single-row-full-theorem), [limits](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#single-row-full-limits) |
| thm:bounded-loading-switching | Single-row full-class readers on arbitrary rows with loading Lambda(F) = sum over rows of the fraction of labels consistent with a class at most lambda_0 have fewer than (2c+2+max(4e lambda_0,(c+1)log n))2^(c+3) heavy queries on every path except with probability 3n^(-c-1): few hosting rows are residual, few matched rows are loaded (negative association), and each assigned row's survivor set in the flat is bounded by affine rank | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by lem:uniform-bijection-row-hit-tail (reviewed) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bounded-loading-theorem) |
| lem:out-loading-satisfaction | Given the flat, the probability that no term is satisfied is at most exp(-sum over rows of the number of labels outside the flat consistent with a class of that row whose label is outside the flat, divided by n+1) | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by lem:out-loading-satisfaction-with-slack (reviewed) | [Lemma and residual case](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#out-loading-lemma) |
| obs:full-class-holes-are-labels | For full-class readers trigger holes lie in the reader label set inside Q; on the both-endpoints tree they are distinct rounds. For at most n^(2/3) labels, the corrected threshold is 2^(3c+4) rounds with failure at most n^(-c-1) in the stated asymptotic regime. | working_proof; corrects by audit:small-label-rank-union-budget (reviewed); corrects by cor:rank-step-correction (reviewed) | [Observation and residual case](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#label-set-observation) |
| thm:single-row-full-class-window | Every mixed reader whose classes are full (all rows pinned to one label) with a single-row light tail, on arbitrary rows, has fewer than N/24 heavy queries on every path before a 1-leaf except with probability 8n^(-c-1), for N at least 2^(c+11)(c+2)^2 ln^2 n; six exclusions organized by labels (heavy labels in the flat and few, large survivor sets outside the flat, light out-loading forces a satisfied term, the flat's light loading is small by charging light-good pairs to light labels, few matched and few residual rows yield light holes) | conditional; corrects by audit:full-class-empty-good-charge (reviewed); corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed); corrects by thm:full-single-row-rounds-with-slack (reviewed) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#srf-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#srf-scope) |
| ex:mixed-tree-round-trip | The mixed canonical tree (matched-first preference, adaptive heavy rule, hole queries) and its encoding decode correctly on every rich bad path of six exhaustively enumerated role-separated instances (n=8, N=2, 3147 paths), and a constructed reader with a row pinned in one term and light in another fails on 2610 of 4050 and 2130 of 4450 paths | finite_check | [Results](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#mixed-tool-results) |
| ex:adaptive-rule-pigeon-counterexample | A full-column reader with k >= 2 classes labelled in the flat, tails satisfied, and every residual row pinned makes the adaptive tree take N-k+1 pigeon queries on one path, all triggered by the same hole, while nu(B_t) = k; for k = 2 the configuration has probability of order N^2/n^2, so the adaptive-tree heavy bounds fail for c >= 2 | refutation | [Gap and counterexample](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#adaptive-rule-gap), [tool records](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-tool) |
| lem:hole-only-rule | Querying the trigger hole at every heavy round (branches over uncovered residual rows and the empty branch) makes the trigger holes of a path pairwise distinct, so heavy queries number at most the hole count of the alive non-falsified pinned pairs, at most N, each answer among N+2 values; for role-separated readers the light encoding applies with the heavy answers recorded and nothing to recompute | working_proof | [Rule, lemma, affected claims](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-rule) |
| lem:light-row-matching-satisfaction | In a bipartite full-column reader, if the graph of matched light rows against hosted light labels outside the flat has a matching of size 12*2^w(c+1) ln n, some term is satisfied except with probability n^(-c-1): the events "row j consistent with its matched label x_j and x_j occupied by a pinned row" use disjoint rows and columns, so negative association gives the product | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed) | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#matching-lemma) |
| thm:bipartite-full-column-switching | Bipartite full-column readers (pinned rows A, light rows B with at most (n-2N-2)/2 rows, classes A x {x} with a single-row light tail on B) have fewer than N/24 heavy queries on every path of the hole-only tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 8n^(-c-1) for N at least 2^(2c+24)(c+3)^2 ln^2 n, and switch at every polynomial size within the height budget on that tree, whose decoder is verified | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by cor:rank-step-correction (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-scope) |
| ex:hole-only-sparse-failure | The unary reader OR_x [i_0 -> x] with i_0 residual (probability (N+1)/(n+1)) has a path of N heavy queries in the hole-only tree, one pigeon query in the adaptive tree, and one round in the both-endpoints tree | refutation | [Failure](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-sparse-failure), [tool records](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-tool) |
| lem:both-endpoints-rule | Querying the trigger pigeon and then, unless it took the trigger hole, the trigger hole makes the trigger pairs of a path pairwise disjoint, a matching in B_t; rounds at most nu(B_t) <= N, queries at most twice that, answers among (N+e)(N+2) values per round; for role-separated readers the light encoding applies with the answers recorded | working_proof | [Rule, lemma, transfer](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule) |
| lem:two-tier-matching-satisfaction | In a column reader, if the graph of matched light rows against light labels outside the flat hosted by dense classes (at least n/4 pinned rows) has a matching of size 20*2^w(c+1) ln n, some term is satisfied except with probability n^(-c-1) | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed) | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-tier-lemma) |
| thm:two-tier-column-switching | Column readers (pinned rows A, light rows B, classes I x {x} with I in A and a single-row light tail on B) whose classes pin at least n/4 rows or are sparse with the sparse classes at each label pinning fewer than n/(400e(N+1)) rows in total have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 8n^(-c-1) for N at least 2^(2c+29)(c+4)^2 ln^2 n, and switch at every polynomial size within the height budget | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by cor:rank-step-correction (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-tier-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-tier-scope) |
| lem:activated-sparse-matching-tail | If for every label x and every light assignment the activated degree (union over the activated classes at each tail row, summed over tail rows) is below D* = n/(400e(N+1)), then the alive pairs with non-falsified tails have matching number >= N/192 with probability <= 2^(-N/192): the tail events are increasing on distinct rows, so negative association bounds them by a product measure on the light rows, and the symmetric sum over N points closes as in the sparse lemma | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed) | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#activated-sparse-lemma) |
| thm:activation-bounded-column-switching | Column readers whose every label is two-tier (sparse classes pin fewer than D* rows) or activation-bounded (activated sparse degree below D* for every light assignment) satisfy the two-tier theorem's conclusion with the same constants | conditional; corrects by cor:rank-step-correction (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#activation-bounded-theorem) |
| lem:two-stage-exposure-reduction | For an arbitrary column reader, after exposing the flat, the residual pinned rows and the pinned rows' labels, the probability of a path with k rounds and no 1-leaf is at most the sum over k-matchings of alive pairs and class choices of the product over tail rows of the unblocked activation weights (labels consistent with the chosen patterns, in the remaining labels, activating no matched class at that row, plus the residual probability) | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed) | [Reduction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-stage-reduction) |
| conj:general-column-claim | Every column reader (classes I x {x}, I in the pinned rows, single-row light tails, role-separated) of a source of size n^c has fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability O(n^(-c-1)) | conjecture | [Claim](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#general-column-claim), [attempts](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#column-falsification) |
| lem:activated-union-degree-tail | Given the flat and the light rows, if every label's activated union of pinned rows (over the classes whose tail row is residual or carries a consistent label) has fewer than n/(800e(N+1)) rows, the alive activated pairs have matching number >= N/192 with probability <= 2^(-N/192): the residual pinned rows form a uniform set of size at most N+1, and the symmetric sum over N points closes | working_proof | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#activated-union-lemma) |
| thm:row-spread-column-switching | Column readers whose light rows each host classes of fewer than n^(1/3) distinct labels have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 4n^(-c-1), for N >= 2^(c+11)(c+1) log n and 2^w <= n^(1/3)/(2^15 e(N+1)(c+1) ln n), and switch at every polynomial size within the height budget | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by cor:rank-step-correction (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#row-spread-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#row-spread-scope) |
| thm:pattern-bounded-switching | Role-separated readers with single-row light tails and any pinned pairs whose light rows carry at most H_max = n^(2/3)/(3300eN(N+1)J_0) patterns, J_0 = 32*2^w(c+1) ln n + N + 1, have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 3n^(-c-1), for N >= 2^(3c+10)(c+1) log n and 2^w <= n^(1/3), and switch at every polynomial size within the height budget; in particular every such reader of width w <= l/(2 log_2(2l+1)) | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pattern-bounded-theorem), [hosts](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#host-setting), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pattern-bounded-scope) |
| cor:rank-step-correction | The independent-points rank bound (\|X\|N/n)^(d+1) is below n^(-c-1) for \|X\| <= n^(2/3) only when d >= 3c+3, and for \|X\| <= n^(1/3) polylog when d >= 2c+2; four recorded steps used d = c+3 with such sets | working_proof | [Correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#rank-step-correction) |
| thm:multirow-mass-switching | Role-separated readers of pinned rows with light tails on any rows have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf, for N >= 2^(d_eps+6)(c+1) log2 n, d_eps = ceil((c+1)/eps)+1, if (i) at most n^(1-eps) labels have a light union of at least n/(800e(N+1)) pinned rows (probability 3n^(-c-1)), or (ii) tails have at most t rows and total width W and no light row carries light-host mass above M_eps = n^(2-eps)/(1600e(N+1)J_0), J_0 = 2t(2^(W+4t)(c+1) ln n + N + 1) (probability 4n^(-c-1)); they switch within the height budget when the tails lie in the light lemma's range | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#multirow-host-setting), [theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#multirow-mass-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#multirow-scope) |
| cor:multirow-size-switching | Every role-separated reader of pinned rows with light tails of any shape and at most n^c terms, 1 <= c < 2-eps, 800e(N+1) <= n^(2-eps-c), has fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path before a 1-leaf (probability 3n^(-c-1)) and switches within the height budget when its tails lie in the light lemma's range | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#multirow-size-corollary) |
| thm:few-light-rows-switching | Role-separated readers of pinned rows with light tails of any shape on at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 light rows, any pinned pairs and any size, have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 4n^(-c-2) for any c > 0, for N >= 2^(d+7) c' log2 n with r_0 = ceil((c+2)/eps)+1, c' = c+2+r_0, d = ceil(c'/eps)+2; they switch within the height budget when the tails lie in the light lemma's range | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#few-rows-setting), [theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#few-rows-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#few-rows-scope) |
| thm:crowded-rows-exposed-switching | Role-separated readers of pinned rows with light tails on at most t rows (total width W, at most n^c terms) and at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 crowded light rows (light mass above M_eps under the heavy threshold 2(N + n^(1-eps)/N^4)(n+1)) have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 6n^(-c-2), for N >= 2^(d+7) c' log2 n and J_0 <= n^(1-eps)/N^4; they switch within the height budget when the tails lie in the light lemma's range | conditional; corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#crowded-setting), [theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#crowded-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#crowded-scope) |
| cor:crowded-size-switching | Every role-separated reader of size n^c with light tails on at most t rows of total width W and 1600e t (N+1) N^4 J_0 n^c <= n^(3-2eps), that is c < 3-2eps-o(1), has fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path (probability 6n^(-c-2)) and switches within the height budget when its tails lie in the light lemma's range | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#crowded-size-corollary) |
| lem:complete-term-rule | On the complete-term tree (the both-endpoints round first, then every uncovered tail row of the current term whatever the round's outcome) the trigger pairs of a path form a matching in the alive pairs of hosts not falsified by rho' and without a matched pair, and every recorded round bound transfers, with fewer than r_0 rounds added for the theorems that expose light rows | working_proof | [Rule and lemmas](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#complete-term-rule) |
| lem:two-role-decoder | With code labels avoiding the moved row's pinned labels, the extension encoding of the complete-term tree decodes for every reader, two-role rows allowed; richness' holds with probability 1 - Mr exp(-e 2^(-w-5)) under sparse pinning (a row pinned to at most a quarter of any pattern cube on it) and e >= 2^(w+3) s | working_proof | [Decoder and richness](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-decoder), [runs](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#complete-term-tool) |
| thm:two-role-few-rows-switching | Readers with at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 light rows, two-role rows allowed, tails of any shape, any pinned pairs, any size, have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the complete-term tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 4n^(-c-2), with N as in `thm:few-light-rows-switching`; under sparse pinning they switch within the height budget | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-few-rows-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#complete-term-scope) |
| thm:two-role-exposed-switching | Two-role readers (tails on at most t rows, at most n^c terms) on the complete-term tree, with heavy threshold 2(N + 2n^(1-eps)/N^4)(n+1), an exposed set of at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 light rows containing every crowded row, and light mass at most n^(2-eps)/(3200e(N+1)) on the two-role rows outside it, have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path before a 1-leaf except with probability 6n^(-c-2), for N >= 2^(d+7) c' log2 n and J_0 <= n^(1-eps)/N^4; under sparse pinning they switch within the height budget | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-many-setting), [theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-many-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-many-scope) |
| cor:two-role-size-switching | Every sparsely pinned two-role reader of size n^c, 1 <= c < 2-eps with 800e(N+1) <= n^(2-eps-c), whatever its tails, has fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the complete-term tree (probability 3n^(-c-1)) and switches within the height budget in the light lemma's range; with tails on at most t rows and J_0 <= n^(1-eps)/(2N^4), the same holds when 3200e t(N+1)N^4 J_0 n^c <= n^(3-2eps) provided its two-role rows number at most n^(1-eps)/(2N^4) or carry light mass at most n^(2-eps)/(3200e(N+1)) | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); corrects by obs:composition-gap (reviewed) | [Corollaries](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-many-corollary) |
| obs:composition-gap | The composition of a heavy-round bound with the light encoding into switching within the height budget, recorded only as the height-budget sketch, is unproved: the round bounds are proved for the compact family Phi_0, the extension encoding exists only on the slack family Phi_e, the recorded mixed-tree rule does not define the slack tree at a pin whose hole is a free outside label, the tool's rounds at such pins have matching number above N for c >= 2 - 2 theta, and the sketch's own arithmetic covers per-row width (2/7) log2 n, not (1/3) log2 n | working_proof | [Gap](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#composition-gap-statement), [status](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#composition-gap-status), [routes](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#composition-gap-routes) |
| lem:dichotomy-switches-single-label-pins | A reader of terms [i->x] & tail (tails of any width) or pin-free light terms with at most w literals per row in the light lemma's range, at most r rows per term, M terms of which M_h pinned, two-role rows and any pinning allowed, has under a uniform compact restriction a matching decision tree over Q of height below s whose 0-leaves falsify every pin-free term while every pinned term is falsified or vanishes, except with probability A^s + Mr exp(-e 2^(-w-4)) + M_h r (N+1)(N+2r)/n^2 (height 0 and the last term alone when every term is pinned); a source of total size n^c, c < 2, switches under one restriction with height O(log S) | working_proof | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#dichotomy-restores-lemma), [composition analysis](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#dichotomy-restores-composition) |
| lem:pair-space-restriction | Pair space with the filling on a fixed e-set S of rows outside every tail: rho uniform in Phi_e(S), rho'' a uniform bijection S -> E(rho); rho' = rho u rho'' is uniform in Phi_0(S), and Pr over Phi_0 of any event is at most (7/5) times its average over S of Pr over Phi_0(S) when e(N+1) <= n/8 (Lemma A0); the slack tree (complete-term tree on the slack instance, hole queried after every pigeon answer, pin test at a pin with a free hole) restricted by the filling is a valid matching decision tree for rho' whose height is the number of real queries (Lemma A) | working_proof | [Setting and lemmas](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-setting) |
| lem:pair-space-decoder | The encoding (rho*, sigma, beta, delta) of the restricted slack tree, rows of S recorded by nothing, code labels in E(rho) n C(p) \ X, is injective (Lemma B); the bad fraction is at most A''^s (2N(N+2))^nu_0 with A'' = 2rN(N+1)e/(n-N-e+1), the family ratio prod (N+1-i)(e-i)/(n-N-e+1+i) paying only for the N+1 compact residual rows (Lemma C); richness' needs sparse pinning, \|X n C(p)\| <= \|C(p)\|/4 | working_proof | [Encoding, decoder, count](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-encoding), [runs](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-tool) |
| lem:pair-space-round-bound | Steps (H1) to (H3) of `thm:multirow-mass-switching` hold on Phi_e(S) with S uniform in A: heavy threshold 2(N+2e)(n+1), D_2^(e) = n/(1600 e_Euler (N+1+e)), union bound (4(N+1+e)/n)^k; at most N/48 - 1 rounds on every path of the slack tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 3n^(-c-1) when the labels with a large light union number at most n^(1-eps), in particular for readers of size n^c when 1600 e_Euler (N+1+e) <= n^(2-eps-c) | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed) | [Lemma E](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-round-bound) |
| thm:pair-space-composition | Composition on the pair space: a sparsely pinned role-separated reader (at most n^c terms, \|X_L^(e)\| <= n^(1-eps) or 1600 e_Euler (N+1+e) <= n^(2-eps-c), tails with 2^(w+3) s <= e <= n/(16 r N^(3/2)(N+1)), N >= 8(c+1) log2 n + (2r+1)(16 + 4 log2 N) and as in the multi-row theorem) admits under a uniform rho' in Phi_0 a matching decision tree over Q of height below N/8 - 2r with correct leaves, except with probability 7n^(-c-1); light range up to width (2-eps-c) log2 n - O(log N) through the size route | conditional; corrects by audit:permutation-NA-source-error (reviewed); supersedes by thm:compact-pair-space-composition (reviewed) | [Theorems D and F](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-round-bound) |
| obs:dense-pinning-obstruction | For readers whose pins cover more than a quarter of some tail pattern cube, the pair-space decoder cannot tell a pin at a free code label from a dead pin (the tool's first design fails off G on 895 of 4770 and similar); naming code labels or slots costs more than the family ratio gains; such readers, and two-role readers, have no composition; the satisfied-term and exposure steps are not transferred | working_proof | [Section 4](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-obstruction) |
| lem:compact-pair-space-decoder | The extension encoding of the compact complete-term tree T_c(F, rho') on the pair space (filling on an e-set S of rows outside every tail; code labels in E(rho) n C(p) whose own filling row is not pinned to them; sigma, positions, answers) is injective: the decoder simulates T_c from rho*, sigma and a mark for the rows filled at code labels, treating a pin at an outside label as satisfied exactly when the row's known label is that label; the bad fraction among rich pairs is at most A''^s (2N(N+2))^nu_0, A'' = 2rN(N+1)e/(n-N-e+1) | working_proof | [Code and decoder](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-code), [runs](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-tool) |
| lem:own-row-richness | For a column-sparse reader (in every tail pattern cube at most a quarter of the labels of C(p) n O are pinned by more than \|A\|/64 rows) with w <= l - l_2 - 1 and 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/16, the pair fails to have s valid labels for some tail pattern with probability at most n 2^(-e/29) + 2Mr exp(-s/3); a reader with at most n^c terms is column-sparse whenever 2^w <= n^(2-c)/1024 | working_proof | [Lemma R](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-richness) |
| thm:compact-pair-space-composition | Composition on Phi_0 for the recorded tree: a column-sparse role-separated reader with tails 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/(16 r N^(3/2)(N+1)) whose compact complete-term tree has at most N/48 - 1 rounds on every path before a 1-leaf except with probability p_R switches within the height budget except with probability p_R + (7/5)(n 2^(-e/29) + 2Mr exp(-s/3) + 2^(-N/8 + (2r+1)(2 + log2 N/2))), i.e. p_R + 5n^(-c-1) under the stated bounds on N, s, e; hence the switching clauses of `cor:multirow-size-switching` (size n^c, c < 2-eps, tails 2^w <= n^(2-c)/1024, no other hypothesis), `cor:crowded-size-switching`, `thm:few-light-rows-switching` and the combined theorem are restored for role-separated readers under column-sparseness, with light range up to width log2 n - O(log N) | working_proof; supersedes by thm:current-term-composition (reviewed) | [Theorem G and corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-remaining) |
| obs:dense-cube-obstruction | Readers above size n^(5/3) whose pins fill a tail pattern cube of width in the light range are not column-sparse: the own-row rule leaves no valid code label for that pattern, and Lemma R fails; such readers' trees are short for other reasons (a consistently matched tail row satisfies a term), so a different argument is needed; two-role readers also remain, a two-role row at a code label changing the status of its own pins in the code | working_proof | [Section 5](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-remaining) |
| thm:two-role-compact-composition | Two-role form of `thm:compact-pair-space-composition`: with code labels outside the moved row's own pinned labels and outside its filling row's pins, the decoder simulates the compact complete-term tree for two-role readers (the current term's alive pinned row is assigned by its round, so it is never moved later); for sparsely pinned, column-sparse readers with \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2 and tails 2^(w+6) s <= e <= n/(16 r N^(3/2)(N+1)), any recorded two-role round bound p_R gives switching within the height budget except with probability p_R + 5 n^(-c-1); hence `thm:two-role-few-rows-switching`, `thm:two-role-exposed-switching` and `cor:two-role-size-switching` regain their switching clauses | working_proof; supersedes by thm:current-term-composition (reviewed) | [Decoder](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-compact-decoder), [richness](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-compact-richness), [theorem and corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-compact-theorem) |
| lem:current-term-decoder | The pair-space code of the compact complete-term tree with the current-term rule (the code label of the k-th move lies in E(rho) n C(p) outside the pin labels of the current terms of the nodes up to and including the move's node; two-role: also outside the moved row's own pinned labels) is injective: a term carrying a pin (f_k, y_k) of an undecoded slot that is not falsified is pin-matched, so it is the current term, which the rule excludes, or comes after it; once the k-th move is decoded the decoder assigns the filling row f_k its label y_k = rho*(j_k) | working_proof | [Decoder](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-decoder), [tool](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-tool) |
| thm:current-term-composition | Composition on Phi_0 without column-sparseness: with the current-term rule, richness is the count \|E(rho) n C(p)\| >= 3s for every tail pattern (failure Mr exp(-s)), and any recorded round bound p_R on the compact complete-term tree gives switching within the height budget except with probability p_R + 3n^(-c-1), for tails 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/(16 r N^(3/2)(N+1)), s >= nu_0, N >= 8(c+1) log2 n + (2r+1)(16 + 4 log2 N), s >= (2c+1) ln n + ln r, with no hypothesis on the pins of a role-separated reader (two-role readers: sparse pinning on their light rows and \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2); hence the switching clauses of `cor:multirow-size-switching`, `cor:crowded-size-switching`, `thm:few-light-rows-switching`, `thm:multirow-mass-switching`, `thm:two-role-few-rows-switching`, `thm:two-role-exposed-switching` and `cor:two-role-size-switching` hold in the pair-space light range without column-sparseness or width conditions | working_proof | [Observation](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-observation), [richness](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-richness), [theorem and corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-theorem), [remaining](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-remaining) |
| thm:wide-move-composition | Composition with wide tail rows (more than w literals, up to l): a wide row moved on the encoded path stays residual in rho* (kind and position in beta, answer in delta), the decoder of `lem:current-term-decoder` simulates the complete-term tree unchanged, and the count becomes (nu_1+1) A_w^(s-nu_1) (4rN)^nu_1 (2N(N+2))^nu_0 with A_w = 4rN(N+1)e/(n-N-e+1); for e <= n/(32 r N^4 (N+1)) and 2^(w+4) s <= e, if every path has at most nu_0 - 1 rounds and at most nu_1 = floor(N/48) wide moves except with probability p_R + p_W, the reader switches within the height budget except with probability p_R + p_W + (7/5)(Mr exp(-s) + 2^(-N/8 + (2r+2)(2 + 3 log2 N))) | conditional; supersedes by thm:wide-round-composition (reviewed) | [Composition](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-composition), [remaining](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-remaining), [checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-tool) |
| obs:shared-wide-cube | For the pin-free reader of the n/2 terms [j in p] with one pattern p of l-1 literals, the complete-term tree has a path of length min(\|R' n B\|, N-1) whenever the flat contains exactly one label of C(p) and no row of B is matched into C(p), an event of probability at least N/(4n) together with \|R' n B\| >= N/8; a hole query at that label gives height one, so the complete-term tree, not the reader, fails per reader at the dichotomy's level n^(c-2), and wide rows need hole queries (a wide round by a smaller-side rule) with a wide-round bound; skipping terms with a residual wide row whose cube misses the flat is also necessary (the reader with distinct near-full patterns queries every residual row) | working_proof | [Lemma and examples](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-obstruction), [tool](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-tool) |
| lem:wide-annihilation | On the wide-round tree a term whose residual wide row j (pattern p) is left unassigned while every hole of C(p) n Q is resolved is annihilated: the image of [j in p] is the sum of X_jx over x in C(p) n Q, and each summand times the leaf factor is a column exclusion or X_jx u_x(pi_0) of the hole-query nodes lemma; a cube missing Q gives the zero image, so the static skip is built in, and the q-skip refutation does not apply because only unplaced rows are skipped | working_proof | [Lemma W0](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-tree) |
| lem:wide-cover-bound | Wide-round tree: with a canonical minimum vertex cover C of the wide graph G (residual wide rows against the holes of Q in their pattern cubes, all terms) fixed at the root, wide rows of C queried as pigeons and the others by hole queries at their cube's unresolved holes, every path has at most \|C\| = nu(G) wide queries (Konig), each with at most N+2 answers; deterministic, in place of a per-node smaller-side rule, which the adaptive-rule counterexample forbids | working_proof | [Lemma W1](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-cover), [tool](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-tool) |
| lem:wide-matching-bound | Pr[nu(G) >= k + t - 1] <= (\|Heavy\| N/n)^(d+1) + (e D N (N+1)/(t(n+1)))^t with d = ceil(log2 k), Heavy the holes of wide degree above D, the wide degree of x counting the wide incidences whose cube contains x; polynomial form: wide mass at most n^(2-2 eps)/(N+1), D = n^(1-eps)/(N+1), d = ceil(c'/eps)+2, t = ceil(c'/eps)+1 give Pr[nu(G) >= 2^d + t - 1] <= n^(-c') for polylogarithmic N and large n; at most n^c terms with wide patterns of at least w_1 literals, 2^(w_1) >= r(N+1) n^(c-1+2 eps), satisfy the mass condition | working_proof | [Lemma W2](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-matching) |
| lem:wide-rounds-transfer | Lemma 1 of the complete-term rule (trigger pairs of rounds pairwise disjoint, host not falsified by rho' and without a matched pair) holds on the wide-round tree, so by Lemma 2 of that rule the multi-row theorem and its size corollary (fewer than N/48 rounds except with probability 3n^(-c-1) for role-separated readers of size n^c, c < 2 - eps) hold on it, confirmed by re-reading steps (H1) to (H4); the few-light-rows, combined and two-role bounds are not transferred (a host can leave the consistent family through a wide row left uncovered) | working_proof | [Lemma W3](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-rounds), [remaining](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-remaining) |
| thm:wide-round-composition | Composition on the wide-round tree with the current-term rule: wide rows never placed, one bit per node and the wide answers in delta, the decoder recomputing the cover from rho*; count 2 A_w^s (2N(N+2))^(nu_0) (2(N+2))^(nu_1) with A_w = 4rN(N+1)e/(n-N-e+1), s = floor(N/12) - 2r - nu_1, nu_0 = nu_1 = floor(N/48), N >= 48(2r+1), 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/(32 r N^4 (N+1)); if every path has at most nu_0 - 1 rounds except with probability p_R and nu(G) <= nu_1 except with probability p_W, the reader switches within the height budget except with probability p_R + p_W + (7/5)(Mr exp(-s) + 2^(-N/8 + (2r+2)(2 + 3 log2 N))); supersedes `thm:wide-move-composition` | conditional | [Theorem W](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-composition), [checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-tool) |
| cor:pin-free-split-rows | A pin-free reader with at most n^c terms whose rows split into light rows (patterns of at most w literals, 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/(32 r N^4 (N+1))) and wide rows of wide mass at most n^(2-2 eps)/(N+1) (every wide pattern of at least w_1 literals with 2^(w_1) >= r(N+1) n^(c-1+2 eps)), \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2, switches under a uniform rho' in Phi_0 within the height budget except with probability 4n^(-c-1), for N polylogarithmic as stated and n large; the light and wide ranges overlap for c < 2 - 2 eps, so then every width is covered per row; mixed rows are absorbed up to n^(1-2 eps)/(2(N+1)) narrow incidences | working_proof | [Corollary 1](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-corollaries) |
| cor:split-rows-below-n2 | Every role-separated reader of size n^c, 1 <= c < 2 - eps, whose tail rows split as in `cor:pin-free-split-rows`, switches under a uniform rho' in Phi_0 within the height budget except with probability 7n^(-c-1), under the multi-row theorem's conditions on N and 800e(N+1) <= n^(2-eps-c); for c < 2 - 2 eps every width is covered per row, closing the window case of wide rows for role-separated readers with split rows at sizes below n^(2-2 eps) | working_proof | [Corollary 2](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-corollaries), [remaining](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-remaining) |
| lem:wide-tree-reordered | The wide-round tree with its node reordered (the current term's wide rows first, then the round only if the term is still alive with an alive pin, then the light moves) keeps Lemmas W0 to W5, Theorem W and both corollaries of the wide-rounds entry with the same proofs, and coincides with the complete-term tree on readers without wide rows; from 2026-09-18 on T_w denotes the reordered tree | working_proof | [Lemma R0](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-exposed-tree), [checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-exposed-tool) |
| lem:exposed-facts-wide-tree | For any exposed set B_e of tail rows and the labeling y_P of the residual exposed rows (assigned rows keep their labels, unassigned rows of a cut last node's term at or after its round get a label of the pattern, all other unassigned rows get the last round's trigger hole): (a) every round's host is consistent with zeta_y on B_e except rounds charged to a light exposed row moved inconsistently at the round's node, at most \|R_e\| of them; (b) a consistent host with a matched pair has no round with its reduced tail, and no round at all when its rows outside B_e are matched consistently | working_proof | [Lemma X](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-exposed-facts) |
| thm:round-bounds-on-wide-tree | The few-light-rows theorem (4n^(-c-2)), the combined theorem and its size corollary (6n^(-c-2)), the two-role few-rows theorem (4n^(-c-2)), the two-role exposed theorem and its Corollary 2 (6n^(-c-2)) and the two-role Corollary 1 (3n^(-c-1)) hold on the reordered wide-round tree, hence every round bound of clauses (a) to (g) of `thm:current-term-composition`'s corollary; their proofs use of the tree only the two facts (`lem:exposed-facts-wide-tree`), Lemma 1 (`lem:wide-rounds-transfer`) and the 1-leaf rule, with the charged rounds absorbed as in Lemma 2 of the complete-term rule | working_proof | [Theorem T](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-exposed-transfer) |

| obs:subspace-design-attempt-obstructions | A nonzero weight vanishing outside a subspace of codimension a has bit degree at least a, hence unary degree at least a/l, above the base barrier at codimension 0.7 v; a uniform subspace of codimension a contains no two points differing in at most r rows except with probability C(n+1,r) 2^(r l - a), hence no product of label sets with two points, so the recorded tensor-product designs (signed cubes, row trades) have no support inside it, while the affine restriction behind Corollary SL reserves two holes per equation | working_proof | [Attempt](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#subspace-residual-attempt), [decision](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#subspace-residual-remaining) |
| lem:parity-tree-bounds-bit-degree | Lemma T: over F_2, a parity decision tree of depth d >= l for the collision search on an affine subspace W gives a Nullstellensatz certificate, hence a PC refutation, of the collision-flat system of bit degree at most d; D_b(W) <= max(l, depth) and <= max(l, dim W) | working_proof | [Lemma T](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wall-review-wall) |
| cor:compact-bit-base-degree-order-n | The compact bit base has PC bit degree between n/2+1 (recorded) and 2n-2+l (halving decision tree), and every affine subspace W has D_b(W) <= min(max(l, dim W), 2n-2+l); exact values 3 and 5 on four and eight holes | working_proof | [Corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wall-review-wall) |
| cor:compact-family-generic-subspace-incompatible | Corollary U: for a uniform affine subspace W of codimension a >= (N+1) l + log2 C(n+1,N+1) + t, except with probability 2^(-t) every restriction of the compact family leaves at most one point of W; on the union source the restriction for the literal reader and the generic equations for the twisted source cannot be combined | working_proof | [Corollary U](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wall-review-falsification) |
| ctx:route-review-subspace-wall | Route review: the value method's hardness inputs, the union source and the unary parities of the simplest recorded unexcluded class (one OR over parities of PHP variables at middle rank) all need PHP hardness under linear constraints of rank of order v, while recorded and published techniques stop at rank of order n; the extension-variable methods of Dahiya-Mahajan-Mouli and Impagliazzo-Mouli-Pitassi need unnested definitions, (M + mn) kappa k = o(n^2), a formula surviving weight-pk assignments and the loss of kappa holes per round, an XOR lift, and p > 2 | context | [Review](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-18-route-review-subspace-wall) |
| lem:generic-subspace-row-width | Lemma L: for a uniform affine subspace W of dimension k, r l + log2 C(n+1,r) + t <= k (then r-2 <= n-1), except with probability 2^(-t) every parity decision tree solving the collision search on W has a path whose queries, in every representation, jointly touch more than r-2 rows; hence depth > (r-2)/omega for queries of row width omega, order n for ordinary decision trees at codimension 0.7 v | working_proof | [Lemma L](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-tree-prover) |
| lem:density-delayer | Lemma D: if a parity decision tree of depth d solves the collision search on W, then for every nonempty G in W some affine subspace U of W inside a collision flat, of codimension s <= d, has \|U cap G\| >= 2^(-s) \|G\|; with G the points of image size n+1-j the prover needs a density boost (n+1)/(2 cbar_j) of near-injective labelings on an affine subspace of the flat | working_proof | [Lemma D](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-tree-delayer) |
| check:near-injective-spectra | Exact Walsh spectra on four and eight holes: bijections have coefficient 1 on the n-1 uniform characters (all labels sum to zero), so boost n at codimension l; image size n-1 has largest coefficient 1/(n-1) (closed form on the uniform characters, maximal on four and eight holes); on eight holes no subspace of codimension <= 3 reaches the needed boost 2.0625, hence parity-decision-tree depth >= 7 for the full bit PHP on eight holes | finite_check | [Spectra](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-tree-spectra), [remaining](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-tree-remaining) |
| lem:sequential-boost-bound | Lemma N: for an affine subspace H whose forms touch tau rows, revealing those rows in any order, the density boost of injective labelings on H is at most the product over pivot positions i of n/(n-i+1), at most n^tau (n-tau)!/n!, with equality on the birthday subspace (and on the sum-zero subspace); for labelings with exactly one colliding pair the same with n+3 (the proof gives n+2) in place of n | working_proof | [Lemma N](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-concentration-sequential) |
| ctx:parity-tree-line-parked | Outcome of the two-entry parity-tree step: no strategy for unrestricted parity queries; absolute-value spectral arguments cannot pass codimension l sqrt(n/2) (heuristically about l^(3/2)); a mixed programme would end near (l^2 n)^(1/3); the transfer would reduce to the same signed sums over characters of large row width (not carried out; only the one-character second moment of the coefficients of G on W is proved); the parity-tree line and the value method (Claim G) are parked | context | [Attempts](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-concentration-wide), [decision](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-concentration-decision) |
| lem:constant-row-clamp | Lemma K: substituting constants for the coefficient rows of complete first-level blocks maps P_b to Q_b = prod_u (1 - lambda_{b,u}), lambda in the span of the block; hence no degree-D PC refutation of the blocks if F with the axioms g_{b,i} Q_b has none, and none of a source with pure readers above if F with the axioms Q_b has none; no substitution factor, any rank; the first part over any prime, the second over F_2 | working_proof | [Lemma K](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#form-review-type) |
| obs:few-fresh-blocks-conservative-over-unary-PHP | Over F_2 and N >= 2B-1, an old polynomial t of degree k with a degree-d PC derivation from the functional unary base plus M fresh blocks with old inputs of degree at most delta (any rank and accuracy) lies in I_k of the base when d + M max(delta, k) <= B; so M blocks give no refutation and no old consequence of degree k while d + M max(delta, k) <= (N+1)/2 | working_proof | [Remark](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#form-review-needs) |
| obs:twisted-majority-hitting-sets | For the recorded approximate-majority parameters every hitting set of the terms has density above 0.47 with high probability, so every point allowed by the term values Z_c = 0 lies on a coordinate subspace of codimension above 0.47 v; term values move the Conjecture H1 instance from dimension 0.3 v to at most about 0.53 v | working_proof | [Falsification (1)](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#form-review-falsification) |
| ctx:route-review-general-step-form | Route review: every form with a recorded polynomial-calculus exclusion at a degree not tied to the accuracy is a design of an enlarged old base composed with old images, needing hardness H(E); forms outside the type (source-module moment designs, fresh-block conservativity, proof-specific forms) with their recorded status; table of what each method pays for M blocks of window rank in the one-level unary family; statement (HW): the unary base with M <= N^c generic avoidance axioms of width h, M 2^(-h) < 1, has no PC refutation of polylogarithmic degree; selected for at most two entries at the NS level | context | [Type](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#form-review-type), [needs](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#form-review-needs), [selection](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#form-review-selection) |
| lem:no-fall-criterion | Lemma E: for the functional unary base over F_2 in matching normal form with clauses Q_b of degree h and G_e = I_e + sum_b Q_b P_{<=e-h}: if G_B cap P_{<=B-1} = G_{B-1} the normal-form PC closure at degree B is G_B; if this holds at every level 1..B then 1 is not in G_B and there is no PC refutation of degree B; no relation between N and B is used | working_proof | [Lemma E](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hw-ns-criterion) |
| obs:forced-degree-fall | Observation F: for X in Y in P_{<=e}, dim X - dim(X cap P_{<=e-1}) <= dim top_e(Y); so a fall at level e is forced once dim G_e - dim G_{e-1} > m_e, and for forms in a space L once the multiples with multipliers from L exceed the top-degree part of I_{h+1} + L^{h+1} | working_proof | [Observation F](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hw-ns-criterion) |
| check:unary-avoidance-ns-spaces | Exact NS spaces over F_2 of the functional unary base with random clauses of h parities (six holes B=3 h=2; seven holes B=4 h=2,3; eight holes B=3 h=2 with general and with decoded bit-affine forms): dim G_B increments c_1-h and c_2-2c_1+3-(b-1) for every clause, 1 enters only at exhaustion (general forms) or at exhaustion of the cubic part of the subalgebra of the forms (label forms, clause 126), first fall exactly at the forced clause (50; 23; 351; 264; label forms 91 with excess 7 = 25*91-2268), except a fall of one at slack 0 or 1 in four of six boundary events; label parity constraints fall first at clauses 19, 19, 20 (degree two; forced at 20, slack one at 19) and 15 (degree three); the recorded PC jumps (50, 91; general parity constraints 15, 32, 17; label parity constraints 19, 19, 20 and 15) are these falls | finite_check | [Table](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hw-ns-data) |
| conj:semi-regular-relative-to-base | Conjecture SR: for uniform forms from a space L (all affine forms, or unary images of bit-affine forms) dim G_B follows H_R(t)(1 - t^h/(1+t)^h)^M, no degree fall occurs before the first clause at which the forced-fall count holds for a pair X = I_e + sum_b Q_b L^d, Y = I_e + L^(d+h) (unary degrees), up to O(1) clauses, and 1 enters only when I_B + L^B lies in G_B; with Lemma E this would give (HW) for generic clauses up to about gamma_B/gamma_{B-h} clauses (order (N^2/B)^h; for label forms a heuristic upper estimate ((N+1)log N/B)^h) | conjecture; corrects by audit:semiregularity-source-scope (reviewed) | [Conjecture SR](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hw-ns-reading) |
| obs:HW-codimension-modulo-rows | In (HW) the codimension of a clause is counted modulo the row sums: the sum of all column sums is constant modulo I_1, so a parity tree that queries it first gives 2^(h-1) clauses of nominal codimension h summing to 1 at degree h; the N onto axioms 1+kappa_j give a degree-one NS refutation over F_2 (classical). A shortcut fails: replacing satisfiable clauses by linear equations at a satisfying value of the forms needs hardness at a chosen right-hand side, which the record has only for safe bit-linear systems (Corollary SL); 1+x_11, 1+x_21 is a counterexample | working_proof | [Falsification attempts](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hw-ns-reading) |
| thm:two-form-no-fall-criterion | Theorem T2: for the functional unary base over F_2 (N >= 3) with parity constraints a_b = l_b + c_b whose linear parts are independent modulo the row sums, if no nonzero element of Kbar (image in Lambda^2 V of the span of products of conflicting cells, dim (N+1)(2N^2-3N+2)/2) vanishes on the annihilator U of the forms, then G_2 cap P_{<=1} = G_1, 1 is not in the degree-two closure, and there is no PC refutation of degree two; fails for one-row forms, column sums (the recorded occupancy equations), and is forced to fail when C(d,2) < dim Kbar | working_proof | [Theorem T2](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-two-criterion) |
| lem:alternating-form-isotropy-bounds | Over F_2: a form of rank 2 rho vanishes on a uniform d-subspace with probability at most 2.39 * 2^(rho(rho+1)/2 - rho d) (Lagrangian count), and at most 6 * 2^((log2 d)^2/6 - C(d,2)) when rho >= d (sequential exposure); Kbar has at most C(n, 2 rho) 2^(2 rho (3N-2)) elements of rank 2 rho (a symmetric matrix of rank r is determined by r rows spanning its row space; each row of a conflict form has at most 2^(3N-2) possibilities) | working_proof | [Lemmas R1, R2, R3](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-two-lemmas) |
| thm:uniform-parity-degree-two | Theorem U: for M independent uniform affine forms and d = N^2-1-M with C(d,2) >= dim Kbar + (log2 d)^2/6 + s and d >= 12N + 8 log2 N + 2s, with probability at least 1 - 2^(-d) - 7*2^(-s) the base with the parity constraints has no degree fall at levels one and two and no PC refutation of degree two; asymptotically M <= N^2 - sqrt2 N^(3/2) (1+o(1)), matching the forced count | working_proof | [Theorem U](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-two-uniform) |
| thm:label-parity-degree-two | Theorem Lb with its structure lemma: for N = 2^l >= 16, Kbar cap (Lbar wedge V) is the direct sum over rows of Lbar_i wedge V_i (on eight holes the constant cross terms remain: 171 = 135 + 36); hence for M uniform decoded bit-affine parity constraints, d_L = (N+1) l - M with C(d_L,2) >= (N+1) C(l,2) + (log2 d_L)^2/6 + s and d_L >= l^2 + l + 4 + 2s, no degree fall and no PC refutation of unary degree two with probability at least 1 - N^2 2^(-d_L) - 9*2^(-s): a uniform affine subspace of the bit space of dimension above about l sqrt(N+1), which includes the case dim W = 0.3 v of Conjecture H1, at degree two only; not sharp for refutations (recorded tipping at dim W = 6, 7) and silent on bit degree | working_proof | [Structure lemma and Theorem Lb](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#parity-two-label) |

| lem:conditioning-defects-are-trades | For arbitrary adjacent old moment-extension sections, conditioning defects land in the zero-restriction trade space; summing one row gives Id-E R, and section changes give M_e L_d-L_(d-1) M_e. | working_proof | [Matching property review and full symbolic proofs](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-matching-property-review) |
| thm:row-predicate-trade-lift | If 1 and s row-label predicates remain independent after any 2d label deletions, their joint conditioning map from K_(d+1) onto s copies of K_d avoiding that row has a linear right inverse; all row bits qualify when N=2^ell>4d. | working_proof | [Joint row lift, source application and exact checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-trade-correction-layer) |
| cor:literal-MP-row-fan-correction | A shared MP fan C_t=(1-a_t)B for robust predicates on one row can be corrected through one more old degree while preserving compatible prescribed B,C_t moments and parallel linear relations. | working_proof | [Joint row lift, source application and exact checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-trade-correction-layer) |
| criterion:two-row-product-code-lift | For two row-evaluation maps, compatible degree-one targets admit a zero-diagonal matching lift exactly when an unrestricted realization has diagonal in the coordinatewise product of the two evaluation kernels; universal lifting has deficit N minus the product-code dimension. | working_proof | [Criterion, obstruction, proof and exact checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-two-row-code-lift) |
| ex:two-row-bit-lift-obstruction | On eight bit labels over every field, one-row robustness and commuting contraction equations hold for explicit two-row degree-one targets that have no common matching lift. | working_proof | [Criterion, obstruction, proof and exact checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-two-row-code-lift) |
| cor:two-row-bit-lift-degree-one | Over any field on the full ell-bit cube with ell>=4, every pair of zero-sum bit-conditioning targets satisfying cross-contraction compatibility admits a degree-two matching lift. | working_proof | [Criterion, obstruction, proof and exact checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-two-row-code-lift) |
| thm:coded-disjoint-trade-spanning | Injection arrays annihilated by row evaluation matrices of ranks q_i are spanned by disjoint codeword tensors of row support at most q_i+1, if every matrix retains rank after sum(q_i+1)-1 label deletions. | working_proof | [Coded spanning, joint lifting and exact checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-coded-trade-spanning) |
| thm:multirow-predicate-trade-lift | For k designated rows with at most s predicates each, targets in K_d avoiding their own row lift jointly to K_(d+1) exactly when cross-contractions agree, if constant-plus-predicate ranks survive 2d+s min(d,k-1) label deletions. | working_proof | [Coded spanning, joint lifting and exact checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-coded-trade-spanning) |
| cor:all-row-bit-trade-lift | On N=2^ell labels over any field, all bit predicates on k designated rows have a common lift of every compatible degree-d defect tuple when N>4d+2ell min(d,k-1); N>2d(ell+2) suffices uniformly in row count. | working_proof | [Coded spanning, joint lifting and exact checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-coded-trade-spanning) |
| cor:multirow-literal-MP-fan-projection | Under the robust joint-lift hypotheses, a shared literal-MP fan extends its prescribed degree-d components exactly when lower equations, same-row boundaries and cross-row closure hold; the projected pair relations have witnesses at the original next-degree budget. | working_proof | [Fan projection, source costs and symbolic obstruction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-multirow-source-closure) |
| obs:lower-fan-data-not-closed | On arbitrarily large robust bit-label boards, lower MP-fan equations and all same-row boundaries can hold while the cross-closure condition fails, even with a normalized prescribed B component. | working_proof | [Fan projection, source costs and symbolic obstruction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-multirow-source-closure) |
| lem:mixed-fan-diamond-closure | With supplied B C_alpha=C_alpha absorption and compatible mixed spectator data, lower shared-fan equations force pair closure; for weights <=L and uniform fan cost 3L a sufficient window is d+w(R)<=D-4L+1. | working_proof | [Mixed-product closure and route review](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-coded-source-route-review) |
| audit:coded-source-closure-route | The coded trade lift solves compatible predicate defects, but a complete-source invariant must still assign mixed components and boundaries while controlling original costs; pause local fan refinements and test a wide MOD2 contraction. | working_proof | [Mixed-product closure and route review](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-coded-source-route-review) |
| thm:wide-row-predicate-complex-exactness | In characteristic two, conditioning by a sum of row predicates on t>d active rows has ker(K_d to K_(d-1)) equal to im(K_(d+1) to K_d), if every constant/predicate evaluation pair survives 3d label deletions; full bit labels suffice when N>6d. | working_proof | [Wide-parity exactness, source application and controls](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-wide-parity-exactness) |
| cor:wide-parity-MP-correction | For a binary old parity on more than d pigeon rows and N>6d, prescribed degree-d B,C components satisfying lower C=(1-a)B equations and aC=0 admit a common B extension through d+1. | working_proof | [Wide-parity exactness, source application and controls](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-20-wide-parity-exactness) |
